## 2026-10-14 - Codex - Diagnose empty local application after pull

**Goal:** Diagnose why pages render but profile, Explore, and mobility data do not load; user explicitly requested no fix and a troubleshooting plan.
**Evidence:** Port 3000 is owned by PID 44288, whose parent chain is plain `npm run dev` started on 13 October at 21:33. It therefore loads `.env.local`, where `NAVIO_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080`. The attempted `npm run dev:vm` briefly selected port 3001 but aborted because the existing Next server already owned the checkout. `netstat` and `.\navio status` show all seven Spring services stopped, including gateway 8080; PostgreSQL, Kafka, Zipkin, and Keycloak containers are healthy, and Keycloak discovery returns 200. Next logs show `/api/users/me` returning 502, Explore upstream fetch failures, and mobility `ECONNREFUSED`, all consistent with the missing gateway. The production environment file points to `https://navio.sit.kmutt.ac.th`, whose certificate-validated `/health` and root both return 200.
**Conclusion:** The pulled source is not the demonstrated cause. The browser is attached to a surviving local-mode Next process while its required Spring backend is stopped. The VM environment file is valid but was never active because the VM-mode Next process could not take ownership. A separate stale Auth.js PKCE error appears in the log and should be retested only after choosing one healthy runtime mode.
**Follow-up blocker:** The user's `.\navio start` attempt failed before any Spring service launched. `docker-compose.storage.yml` requests `minio/minio:RELEASE.2025-04-22T22-12-26Z`; Docker Hub rejects the pull, and Docker has neither a local MinIO image nor container. The runner then waits for MinIO readiness and aborts infrastructure initialization. The production stack already uses `.deploy/docker/community-storage.Dockerfile` to build pinned MinIO and `mc` from source, but the local Compose file was not migrated to that approach.
**Plan:** Added `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-runtime-loading-recovery.md`, separating local (`.\navio start` + `npm run dev`) and VM (`npm run dev:vm` after stopping the verified local server) recovery, then authentication and feature-level verification. The plan now first aligns local storage with the existing production source-built image and verifies health, idempotent bucket setup, and object round-trip. No source/config/service/process/database fix was performed.

## 2026-10-13 - Codex - Diagnose local versus production login identity

**Goal:** Determine why the user's working production account is rejected after pulling and reconstructing the local workspace.
**Evidence:** `.env.local` points Auth.js to local Keycloak on port 8180 and the local API gateway on port 8080. Local Keycloak responds successfully and runs `KC_DB=dev-mem`; production Compose runs Keycloak against PostgreSQL. A read-only admin query found exactly one matching enabled local user with a password credential and no required action. The local Keycloak container started at 21:10, that user was created at 21:13, and the login event was rejected as `invalid_user_credentials`. The local gateway is currently unreachable.
**Conclusion:** This is local environment state, not missing Git code and not a production VM failure. The local password database was recreated and is independent from production. The gateway outage is a separate local runtime issue affecting the application after authentication.

## 2026-10-14 - Claude - Auto plan replaces Plan for me

**Goal:** User said Plan for me was wrong. They want the places they already added to a day (from guides or the map) put in a good order: nearby places together, restaurants at lunch and dinner, every stop open on arrival. They asked for an "Auto plan" button beside the day colour using shadcn, consistent with Calculate times ("auto time").
**Done:** New client folder planner/planId/_components/auto-plan/. Pure planner day-auto-plan.ts runs a time-dependent simulation (opening windows, lunch 11:30–14:00 and dinner 17:30–20:30 start windows, bars after 17:00, return to the end anchor). It uses cheapest insertion from three seeds plus relocate/2-opt local search, under 100 ms at 45 places. Fixed times are anchors and nothing is made late for them. A "Keep the N fixed times" checkbox lets the plan move them. Places that cannot fit (closed that day, hours outside the day, no time left) are listed with a reason. They either stay untimed at the end of the day or move to a chosen day. Notes, checklists, stations and chargers stay after the place they followed; hotels stay last. The dialog (Dialog/Select/Checkbox/Badge/Skeleton) rebuilds live as Start, Finish by and Getting around change. It shows a day ribbon, the travel saved against the current order, a numbered timeline with hours, meal badges and waits, and notes. Place details reuse the ["place-details"] query cache with a 15 s timeout; failures mean unknown hours, never an error. Apply is one atomic jotai write, rejected if the day changed. An inline "Day planned... Undo" notice restores every affected day.
**Auto time integration:** Meal stops, stops waiting for an opening, and a first stop that isn't at 9:00 are saved timeSource=fixed, so Calculate times keeps them. Other stops are calculated. Once routes for the new order load (the query key changes, so they are never the old order's routes), AutoPlanTimeRefiner runs the keep-fixed calculation once with routed travel, so Times does not show Needs updating; Undo still works afterwards. Typical visit lengths moved to itinerary/visit-length.ts and are shared: Calculate times now uses the place type (museum 2 hr) instead of a flat 60 min for stops without an end. Untyped stops keep 60 min. Wording unified on "fixed time": Calculate times hint "Keeps fixed times.", idle "Fixed times stay as they are.".
**Removed:** Codex's Plan for me files (guide/{auto-plan-dialog,auto-plan-preview,auto-plan.atoms,guide-auto-plan,load-auto-plan}.ts(x), tests/planner/auto-plan{.test,.browser}.mjs, tests/planner/auto-plan/data.ts). guide-plan-hours.ts moved to auto-plan/opening-hours.ts. guide-browser-panel, guide.atoms and popular-places were restored to HEAD (their only diff was Plan for me). Backup: the Claude scratchpad plan-for-me-backup folder (temporary). place-api weekdayOpeningHours/signal and types.ts weekdayOpeningHours were kept.
**Verified:** Whole-client tsc and scoped ESLint pass (one existing warning in empty-itinerary-callout.tsx). All 370 node tests pass, including 16 new auto-plan tests: hours, clustering, lunch/dinner, opening waits, unfit reasons, fixed times, grouping, move/keep overflow, apply/undo/stale/read-only, routed refinement, a morning that isn't left empty, and shared visit length. New tests/planner/auto-plan.browser.mjs (all APIs intercepted, no real writes) passes in light 1440 and dark 390. It covers keyboard open, Escape with focus return, apply with the closed museum moved to day 2, the note staying after Tokyo Tower, lunch/dinner saved as fixed, no Needs updating after routes, Undo and no overflow. Run it with NAVIO_PLAYWRIGHT_MODULE pointing at an installed playwright index.mjs. Its "Guide response has an unexpected shape" console errors come from the fixture's generic guides stub.

## 2026-10-14 - Codex - Revise itinerary time calculation proposal

**Goal:** Revise the user's new time-calculation feature: user-edited A/C times must survive calculation of B/D; decide between automatic updates and a Calculate times button with keep/overwrite choices.
**Proposal:** Start with per-day Calculate times, preserving manually edited start/end values as fixed anchors and refreshing calculated values in the existing place order. Separate Recalculate all times exposes a preview of affected manual values and explicit apply; offer Undo. Mark Fixed versus Calculated, let users release fixed values, and show Times need updating after relevant itinerary/route/duration changes. Persist provenance/fixed metadata through autosave/reload; conservatively preserve existing values whose origin is unknown and let the user release them. Calculate from a day start or anchor using visit durations plus route travel durations, retaining waiting gaps. Keep A/C and show a quantitative warning if the interval cannot fit; do not silently shorten visits, move anchors or count missing durations as zero. Later independent intervals can calculate from C. Define partial start/end constraints and prevent stale previews/results from replacing newer edits before implementation.
**Council/checks:** Used llm-council: five independent advisor perspectives, five anonymized peer reviews, root synthesis. All favored the explicit v1 action and preserving manual values; reviewers favored a separate overwrite entry over a recurring popup. Inspected PlaceTimePopover, PlaceItem and planner state/persistence references: current cards save time/timeEnd but have no timing provenance flag. Checked actual root/client/server and changed community/mobility repository state. Consulted Apple's primary alerts/undo guidance. No application tests run because this session only revises the proposal.

Preserve all pre-existing client selected-transit-route edits, tsconfig/CLAUDE.md, r1-r6.json, root notes/report work, and service logs/crash dumps. ## 2026-10-14 - Claude - Saved transit route still "unverified"; Tokyo transit missing

**Goal:** User reported Bangkok leg Nantawan Srinagarindra -> True Tower 1 still showing "Selected route unverified" after Codex's fix, and Tokyo transit (OTP) no longer working.
**Cause (Bangkok):** Mobility caches transit alternatives per leg and per departure *minute* (`RouteService.resolveRoutePlan`, departure = now truncated to minutes). Every planner/chooser search is a fresh Google call for "now", and Google's alternative set (next bus, boarding stop) changes between calls, so the exact saved line/station key often is not returned again. The chooser screenshot confirmed it: the saved journey was not in its list (radio fell back to the top row). Codex's wider search could not fix that.
**Fix (client, uncommitted on fix/selected-transit-route):** `applySavedTransitRoutes` keeps exact matches; otherwise it shows the closest confirmed current journey (most shared lines, then balanced rank) with client-only `RouteSegment.savedRouteUnavailable`. `RouteSegmentInfo` shows "Saved route not offered now" with an explanatory popover; the chooser preselects that journey so "Use this route" can re-save it. "Selected route unverified" now only appears when no transit journey is confirmed at all. Tests updated deliberately (old ones asserted the hide-everything behavior) plus a same-lines-preferred regression.
**Cause (Tokyo):** Next on :3000 runs `npm run dev:vm`, i.e. against the production VM, where OTP is disabled. Locally, gateway/Mobility/OTP (18801) have all been stopped since 2026-10-13 22:27. Graph and jar still exist in C:/Users/User/.codex/tmp/navio-open-transit-20261013/{graph,otp-shaded-2.10.0.jar}. No code change needed.
**Verified:** 337/337 client tests, `tsc --noEmit`, ESLint on routes/. Live replay through the dev server's /api/routes/directions (production Google): all six chooser journeys restore exactly; a stale key returns routed + flagged on the same lines (45 bus > Blue Line). Browser UI not visually checked.
**Not done / left uncommitted:** Nothing committed (root has other sessions' uncommitted handoff/log edits on fix/traveler-guides-route-local-minio). Did not start OTP/services or switch the dev server.
**Follow-ups:** For local Tokyo transit: stop dev:vm, `scripts/transit/start-otp.ps1 -DataDirectory <graph> -JarPath <jar> -Port 18801`, `./navio.cmd start`, then `npm run dev`. Longer-term: persist the chosen journey snapshot or search with the trip's date/time so saved routes survive timetable drift.

## 2026-10-13 - Claude - Reddit-style community redesign with icons, reactions and a create wizard

**Goal:** User asked (frontend-design) to redesign community like Reddit: Reddit's create-community flow, every community picture uploadable, shadcn, more color without overdoing it, and reactions in comments.
**Done (backend, community-service `feat/community-reddit-redesign` from dev, uncommitted):** Additive `V7__group_icons_and_comment_reactions.sql`: nullable `social.group_profiles.icon_media_id` (FK to `media.group_media`) and `social.comment_reactions` (comment, user, reaction; PK on all three; check `helpful|love|funny|thanks|want_to_go`; cascades with the comment). `GroupPictureService` now serves a `GroupPictureSlot` (banner, icon); new `POST/DELETE/GET /v1/groups/{slug}/icon` mirror the banner. Group detail, every list item (discover/mine/search) and every post now carry `iconMediaId`/`bannerMediaId`/`groupIconMediaId`. `PUT/DELETE /v1/posts/{postId}/comments/{id}/reactions/{reaction}` (idempotent; active members; deleted comments rejected; soft delete clears reactions); `CommentResponse.reactions[] {reaction,count,reacted}` loaded with one grouped query per page. `GET /v1/posts?joined=true` gives a Home feed of joined communities (anonymous gets an empty page). `sort=best` is now a real hot ranking (score + comments×0.5, decayed by age) instead of duplicating `top`. Communities show as `n/slug` with a stable trail hue per slug; missing pictures get a generated contour-map banner and lettered icon. New tokens in globals.css: `--vote-up/down`, five `--react-*`, six `--trail-*` (light and dark). Reddit-style three-step create wizard (name/description with address preview → banner and icon with live preview card → topics/country/places), mounted once in the community layout; it creates the group, then uploads banner and icon, and offers retry/continue if an upload fails after creation. ## 2026-10-13 - Claude - Transit filters from actual vehicles, sort defaults to fastest

**Goal:** The fixed "All transit / MRT & rail / Bus" chips should be built from the vehicles the found routes actually use (bus, train, metro, ferry and so on). Add shadcn filter/sort controls for time, walking and similar. The user then asked for the default order to be the shortest time.
**Done:** New `routes/transit-journey-filters.ts` works out each journey's vehicle kinds (Metro, Train, Monorail, Tram, Bus, Ferry, Other), counts the available kinds, filters inclusively (a mixed route appears under each vehicle it rides), and sorts by Fastest (default), Least walking, Fewest transfers, Lowest fare (shown only when at least two routes have a fare) or Balanced (the old score). Ties go to the faster route. `transit-route-choice.ts` now exports duration, walking and transfer metrics, and the score is built from them. New `transit-journey-toolbar.tsx` holds the vehicle chips (aria-pressed Buttons with counts, shown only when there are two or more kinds) and the shadcn Select sort, plus an sr-only result count. The sort lives in Jotai `transitJourneySortAtom`, shared by all legs. `route-journey-options.tsx` always lists the merged "transit" union and filters it on the client. When there is no saved route key, the default selection is the first route in the sorted list that matches a planned rail/bus preference. The Transit mode tab shows the fastest duration. `transit-journey-list.tsx` titles each route by its vehicles ("Metro + Bus"), uses per-vehicle icons, and labels the top route with the sort name instead of "Recommended".
**Verified:** Whole-workspace `tsc --noEmit` and ESLint on `routes/` are clean. ## 2026-10-13 - Claude - Transit stations clickable with Transit lines on

**Goal:** User could not click a station on the Google planner map while the Transit lines toggle was on.
**Cause (reproduced):** A headless probe against the real Maps JS API at Minami-asagaya returned station placeIds without TransitLayer and zero POI hits with it. Google's transit overlay redraws stations without click targets, so `handleMapPoiClick` never received a placeId.
**Done:** New `routes/transit-station-click.ts` converts zoom into a ~40px search radius (null below zoom 14) and returns the nearest station from distance-ranked results. When transit lines are visible and a click has no placeId, `planner-map-google.tsx` calls the existing `/api/geo/places/nearby` and opens that station through `startPlaceSearch`. Normal POI clicks are unchanged. A failed lookup now logs `console.warn` instead of being swallowed silently.
**Verified:** New `tests/planner/transit-station-click.test.mjs` passes, with transit-legs 23/23 combined. TypeScript noEmit and targeted ESLint are clean. The live local nearby endpoint at the measured station icon position returned the `subway_station` second, behind one shop, and the helper picks it. I did not click it in the actual planner UI.

**Follow-ups:** A click on the far end of a long station label (more than ~40px from the icon) still misses. Each empty-map click with transit lines on at zoom 14 or higher makes one nearby request.

## 2026-10-13 - Codex - Diagnose Google Maps versus Tokyo transit API

**Goal:** Answer whether Navio can show Google Maps-style transit alternatives after the user supplied a Tokyo Tower to New National Theatre example that is available in Google Maps but unverified in Navio.
**Done:** Read current provider/proxy code and verified official Google documentation. Public read-only routing requests through both localhost:3000/api/routes/directions and localhost:8080/v1/routes/directions returned transit fallback, reason No public transit route was found, 6012 m direct distance and no steps. Google Maps Platform FAQ explicitly states Routes API excludes Japanese transit partners (also IRCTC). Therefore the Tokyo example has a provider coverage limitation, not simply a missing UI. Google Routes can return up to three alternatives in addition to the main route, station and departure/arrival details, and fares where supplied, in supported regions. Current Navio keeps one provider route, requests alternatives only for rail-only searches, strips schedule metadata, and reports moving time rather than complete scheduled journey time. Google-style route rows need a richer provider/response contract. NAVITIME's official transit API is a viable Japan-capable integration option with separate access and relevant timetable/service options to check.
**Sources:** https://developers.google.com/maps/faq#transit_directions_countries ; https://developers.google.com/maps/documentation/routes/transit-route ; https://api-sdk.navitime.co.jp/api/specs/api_guide/route_transit.html
**Verified:** Current source inspection, official documentation and two live Navio public routing responses. No account/planner writes, new provider account/API key, application edits, restart, schema changes, push or deployment. Prior travel UI remains uncommitted on existing branches. Concurrent auth/guest-removal and development-runner changes were observed and preserved; prior frontend test counts are historical for their tested state.
**Follow-ups:** Extend the common transit journey contract to include alternatives, schedule context, overall elapsed time, walking, lines/stops and nullable fares. Keep Google for covered regions and integrate a licensed Japan-capable transit source for Tokyo; changing the UI or departure time alone cannot overcome the documented Japan exclusion. No new branches, per the user's instruction. Session notes committed on the existing root docs branch and fast-forwarded locally into dev only.

**Request:** Fix Add EV station search failure without changing existing UI or unrelated Phase 4C work.
**Diagnosis:** Existing Promise.all launched every stop/polyline search simultaneously; one rejection discarded all successful results. Error serialization lost HTTP details. Read-only localhost station lookup for Bangkok succeeded against the connected backend; original failing status was not retained in the development log, so the precise original provider/auth/network cause is unconfirmed. No connected browser was available for authenticated reproduction.
**Changed:** Client ev-station-search.ts limits searches to two concurrent requests, deduplicates exact coordinates while retaining anchor identities, retries network/502/503/504/timeout failures once, preserves successful areas and distinguishes partial/total failure from no stations. ev-station-api.ts adds a 20-second request timeout and typed HTTP errors; add-ev-station-button.tsx uses the helper and existing error display with readable diagnostics. JSX/styles unchanged; no routing, energy, backend or account behavior changed.
**Verified:** 136 frontend tests passed (7 new regression tests); TypeScript noEmit, targeted ESLint and diff whitespace pass. Initial regression tests caught a missing break that retried non-transient errors; corrected before the full passing run. Test output: output/ev-station-search-tests.log. No live user-trip modification, push or deployment.
**Next:** User retries Add EV station. If failure persists, the message now contains status; investigate that specific connected service failure. Application fix remains local/uncommitted alongside preserved Phase 4C work. Previous remote transit integration/migration V16/V17 release gate remains unchanged; Phase 5 not started.

Default Node 22.14 cannot run these tests; compatible runtime used without dependency edits. No live authenticated user write replayed.

**Release:** IAM main `5293c22`, server main `416644c`, root main `16fffce`; root dev release `49bfa50`. Child-first dev/main publication; all six pins checked against live origin/main. Client remains published `f790652`, trip `f0549d4`, mobility `eb72b37`, community `5f0cf41`. Authenticated end-to-end adds were covered by controller/service tests, not replayed against a real user account.

## 2026-09-23 â€” Claude â€” Fix the Grafana proxy and turn on production tracing

**Goal:** make Grafana work in production and get Zipkin tracing running there.
**Done:** (all root-repo, branch `dev`, **uncommitted**)
- `.deploy/nginx/navio.conf` â€” confirmed the previous session's hypothesis and fixed it: a `location`-level `proxy_set_header` discards every server-level one, so `/grafana/` was sending `Host: navio_grafana`, and Grafana's CSRF middleware answered 403 to every `POST /api/ds/query`. Re-declared all six inherited headers. Applied the same fix to `location /` (navio-web), which had the identical defect and guards Server Actions the same way.
- `.deploy/compose.production.yml` â€” added an internal `zipkin` service (`openzipkin/zipkin:3.5.1`, `STORAGE_TYPE: mem`, `MEM_MAX_SPANS=50000`, `mem_limit: 320m`, `healthcheck: disable: true` so it can never fail `compose up --wait` and trigger a rollback); replaced the no-op `MANAGEMENT_TRACING_EXPORT_ZIPKIN_ENABLED` with `MANAGEMENT_TRACING_EXPORT_ENABLED` plus `MANAGEMENT_TRACING_EXPORT_ZIPKIN_ENDPOINT`; sampling is now `${NAVIO_TRACING_SAMPLING_PROBABILITY:-0.1}`; `GF_SERVER_ROOT_URL` default corrected to `https://`.
- `.deploy/observability/prometheus.yml` â€” `zipkin` scrape job mirroring the dev stack's.

## 2026-09-22 â€” Claude â€” LLM council: EV calibration and adoption

**Goal:** the professor asked how battery differences are calibrated (the user answered "we use consumption rate"); the user asked what to improve in the EV feature, whether NEDC/WLTP etc. matter, and how to make real EV drivers want it.
**Done:** advice only, via a 5-advisor council with peer review. No code changes.
**Verdict:** the consumption answer is half right. Consumption covers energy, but the number comes from unsourced factors (NEDC/CLTC 0.7, WLTP 0.85, EPA 0.9, plus the 1.12 margin, 12% reserve and 0.9 charge efficiency), and one charge curve for every car ignores LFP vs NMC. Next: (1) run the flow in the browser with live routes; (2) a small evaluation: fixed Thai routes Ã— cars against published real-world figures, pass/fail thresholds set before measuring, plus a Â±15% consumption sensitivity rerun that checks whether the charging stops change; (3) sourced real-world consumption and usable kWh per catalog car, with test-cycle factors only as a cited fallback; (4) HVAC as a per-hour load (route duration Ã— kW); (5) LFP/NMC charge-curve profiles. Position Navio as a trip planner with charging built into the itinerary, not as a physics rival to ABRP.
**Verified:** n/a (no code).
**Not done / left uncommitted:** this entry and the handoff note. `handoff.md` already had uncommitted edits at session start, so nothing was committed.
**Follow-ups:** the user to choose which steps to implement. A change to the catalog schema needs a new migration (see `database-changes.md`) and regression tests on the optimizer before recalibrating.

## 2026-10-11 - Claude - Notion-style block editor for planner notes

**Goal:** User asked to rework the markdown notes editor into a full Notion-style editor (slash/+ block menu, movable blocks, selection toolbar, ⋯ block actions menu), using frontend-design.
**Done:** Replaced the textarea editor (components/markdown/, deleted along with its test) with `client/components/block-editor/` on TipTap 3.31.4 (ProseMirror). "/" or the gutter "+" opens a filtered block menu (text, H1-H4, bulleted/numbered/to-do/toggle lists, quote, callout, code, divider) with arrow keys/Enter/Esc; markdown shortcuts (#, -, 1., [], >, ```) also work. Hovering a block shows "+" and a grip: drag moves the block (drop line drawn as a route segment with a waypoint), click opens block actions (search, turn into, color text/background, duplicate, move up/down, delete). Selecting text shows a toolbar: turn into, B/I/U/S/code, link, color, ⋯ (same block menu). Keyboard: Ctrl+/ block menu, Ctrl+D duplicate, Ctrl+Shift+Up/Down move, Ctrl+K link, Alt+F10 toolbar, Ctrl+Enter tick to-do. Notes render statically (identical CSS) and swap to the lazily loaded editor on the first press/focus, replaying the press so the caret lands where clicked. Storage stays markdown in `block_item.notes` (GitHub conventions: `> [!TIP]` callouts, `<details>` toggles, `<u>`, `<span|mark data-color>`; any other HTML stays literal text; unsafe links dropped), so no server/DTO/migration change. Shared plan view uses the same static renderer. Removed react-markdown/remark-breaks; added @tiptap/* 3.31.4, @floating-ui/dom, unified, remark-parse, @types/mdast.
**Verified:** 20 new converter unit tests (round trips, escaping, legacy plain-text line breaks, safety). Client suite 112/113 (only the known golden-fixture test needing a sibling server dir). TypeScript and changed-file ESLint clean. Chrome (playwright-core from an earlier session's scratchpad, temporary page since removed) 31/32 on the editor plus 4/4 inside the real guest planner (new note, slash blocks, to-do drag, planner card not dragged); the only failures were next-auth 500/backend 503 console errors from a worktree with no .env/backend. ## 2026-10-11 - Claude - Production incident: planner snapshots unreadable; hotfix + TWD release

**Incident:** Release a9fc36f (first production release of Phase 4A + transit) broke every saved-trip load ("The saved trip could not be loaded. Please retry.", dashboard stats failing) and new trips got no days. Cause: trip-planning-service HTTP runs Jackson 3, but `energyState`, `observedSocCheckpoint` and `arrivalMode` are Jackson 2 `JsonNode`s, which Jackson 3 serialized as bean getters (`{"array":false,...,"nodeType":"NULL"}`); the client's schema rejected every snapshot. Introduced with Phase 4A energy fields, never previously released; earlier browser checks used mocked APIs, and slice tests never asserted these fields.
**Fix:** trip ca2befc adds `config/LegacyJsonNodeModule` (Jackson 3 module: writes Jackson 2 nodes as raw JSON; explicit null -> NullNode, omitted -> null). `PlannerJsonContractTests` drives real HTTP both ways and fails 3/3 without the module. Reproduced and verified against the released service locally (real Postgres): empty trip energyState null, save/reload of energy state, observation, placeType and arrivalMode; the client's real `getPlannerSnapshot` accepts the fixed payload and rejects the broken one.
**Also released at user request:** Codex's TWD currency (trip f777e73, client 306ee3e); budget_currency is varchar(3), no migration. Trip 199/199 on real Postgres, client 133/133 + TypeScript + lint.
**Git:** trip main/dev 77d21f3, client main/dev 306ee3e, server main/dev 5956733, root pins pushed to dev and main (deploys). User's checkouts were switched to main earlier this session; two stale 2026-09-26 release worktrees in %TEMP% holding main (only deleted tracked files, no work) were removed.
**Next:** After deploy, confirm a real signed-in trip loads and saves. Consider moving the trip DTOs to Jackson 3 types and removing the module. Phase 4A acceptance (optimize/apply/reopen) is still outstanding.

## 2026-09-23 — Claude — Build the client half of plan link sharing

**Goal:** finish "publish plan as a link", which a previous session had left as a complete backend and no client at all.

**Done:** (client, branch `feat/garage-ev-redesign`, **uncommitted**)
- `app/feature/planner/_components/share/publication-api.ts` — typed mirrors of the server DTOs (`PublicationOptions`, `Publication`, `PublicPlanSnapshot` and its nested day/item/anchor/charger/budget shapes), owner fetchers for get/preview/publish/revoke, and `sharedPlanPath`/`sharedPlanUrl`. Unreadable options parse to "nothing extra was shared", never the reverse.
- `app/feature/planner/_components/share/use-publication.ts` — TanStack Query hooks. `staleTime: 0, gcTime: 0` on the link state so a revocation in another tab cannot be masked by cache.
- `app/feature/planner/_components/planner-autosave-flush.ts` (new) and `planId/_components/overview/planner-persistence.tsx` — the save-flush barrier the plan required. `PlannerPersistence` now records its pending debounced save in `pendingSaveRef` and registers a flush handler keyed by trip id; `usePublishPlan` calls it, then publishes that exact version. Deliberately **not** in the `planner-autosave-<tripId>` mutation scope: the flush calls the autosave mutation, so taking the same scope would deadlock the two.
- `app/feature/planner/_components/share/publish-plan-dialog.tsx` plus `share-option-row.tsx` and `shared-link-field.tsx` — the owner dialog: access summary, three opt-ins (all off by default), inline preview with Back, published state with selectable URL and copy, update-when-changed, and an inline stop-sharing confirmation. Clipboard failure leaves the URL selectable with `role="status"` feedback.
- `app/feature/planner/_components/share/shared-plan-content.tsx` — one renderer for the projected snapshot, used by **both** the owner's preview and the recipient page, so the preview cannot drift from the real page. Takes only the sanitised snapshot.
- `app/share/plans/[token]/page.tsx` and `shared-plan-request.ts` — the recipient page as a **Server Component** fetching the gateway directly, `force-dynamic`, `robots: noindex/nofollow/nocache`, generic metadata so unfurl services retain nothing. One "no longer available" page for every dead-link cause.
- `app/feature/planner/_components/trip-actions-menu.tsx` — entry point above Delete, labelled "Publish plan as a link" / "Manage published link". The publication query is enabled only while the dropdown is open, so the dashboard does not fire one request per trip card.
- Deleted the `app/api/shared-plans/[token]` proxy I had written first: with the page fetching server-side it had no consumer, and an unauthenticated route on our origin with no caller is surface for nothing.

## 2026-09-23 — Claude — Fix the Grafana proxy and turn on production tracing

**Goal:** make Grafana work in production and get Zipkin tracing running there.
**Done:** (all root-repo, branch `dev`, **uncommitted**)
- `.deploy/nginx/navio.conf` — confirmed the previous session's hypothesis and fixed it: a `location`-level `proxy_set_header` discards every server-level one, so `/grafana/` was sending `Host: navio_grafana`, and Grafana's CSRF middleware answered 403 to every `POST /api/ds/query`. Re-declared all six inherited headers. Applied the same fix to `location /` (navio-web), which had the identical defect and guards Server Actions the same way.
- `.deploy/compose.production.yml` — added an internal `zipkin` service (`openzipkin/zipkin:3.5.1`, `STORAGE_TYPE: mem`, `MEM_MAX_SPANS=50000`, `mem_limit: 320m`, `healthcheck: disable: true` so it can never fail `compose up --wait` and trigger a rollback); replaced the no-op `MANAGEMENT_TRACING_EXPORT_ZIPKIN_ENABLED` with `MANAGEMENT_TRACING_EXPORT_ENABLED` plus `MANAGEMENT_TRACING_EXPORT_ZIPKIN_ENDPOINT`; sampling is now `${NAVIO_TRACING_SAMPLING_PROBABILITY:-0.1}`; `GF_SERVER_ROOT_URL` default corrected to `https://`.
- `.deploy/observability/prometheus.yml` — `zipkin` scrape job mirroring the dev stack's.
- `.deploy/observability/grafana/datasources.yml` and `grafana/provisioning/datasources/datasources.yml` — production gains the Zipkin datasource (trace→logs/metrics, copied from the dev stack's working config); both gain a Loki `derivedFields` entry linking the trace id in Spring Boot's `[app,traceId,spanId]` log prefix into Zipkin.
- `.deploy/.env.example` — documented `NAVIO_TRACING_SAMPLING_PROBABILITY`.
- `grafana/dashboards/navio-overview.json` — removed two `http://localhost:` dashboard links that could never work in production.

**Verified:** YAML and JSON parse; the shared Java env anchor was confirmed by parsing the compose file to reach all seven Java services; the dev and production Zipkin scrape jobs differ only in the `environment` label. **Not verified:** nothing was deployed or exercised against the live VM — the 403 root cause, the Zipkin target coming up, the derived-field regex against real log lines, and the trace→logs links are all unconfirmed in a running stack.

**Not done / left uncommitted:** everything above. Untouched and preserved as other sessions' work: the `client` and `server` submodule working trees, `docs/agents/admin-dashboard-plan.md`, `docs/agents/plan-link-sharing-plan.md`, `docs/research/`.

**Follow-ups:** declared `mem_limit` across the stack is now ~8.3 GB — check `free -h` on the VM before releasing. Not fixed because it needs a `server` submodule change: the config server hardcodes `management.tracing.sampling.probability: 1.0` for three services and uses a different env name (`TRACING_SAMPLING_PROBABILITY`) for two others; harmless only while `spring.config.import` config-data mode keeps `systemEnvironment` above config-server values.

## 2026-09-22 — Claude — LLM council: EV calibration and adoption

**Goal:** the professor asked how battery differences are calibrated (the user answered "we use consumption rate"); the user asked what to improve in the EV feature, whether NEDC/WLTP etc. matter, and how to make real EV drivers want it.
**Done:** advice only, via a 5-advisor council with peer review. No code changes.
**Verdict:** the consumption answer is half right. Consumption covers energy, but the number comes from unsourced factors (NEDC/CLTC 0.7, WLTP 0.85, EPA 0.9, plus the 1.12 margin, 12% reserve and 0.9 charge efficiency), and one charge curve for every car ignores LFP vs NMC. Next: (1) run the flow in the browser with live routes; (2) a small evaluation: fixed Thai routes × cars against published real-world figures, pass/fail thresholds set before measuring, plus a ±15% consumption sensitivity rerun that checks whether the charging stops change; (3) sourced real-world consumption and usable kWh per catalog car, with test-cycle factors only as a cited fallback; (4) HVAC as a per-hour load (route duration × kW); (5) LFP/NMC charge-curve profiles. Position Navio as a trip planner with charging built into the itinerary, not as a physics rival to ABRP.
**Verified:** n/a (no code).
**Not done / left uncommitted:** this entry and the handoff note. `handoff.md` already had uncommitted edits at session start, so nothing was committed.
**Follow-ups:** the user to choose which steps to implement. A change to the catalog schema needs a new migration (see `database-changes.md`) and regression tests on the optimizer before recalibrating.

## 2026-10-13 - Codex - Diagnose missing TipTap module

**Request/result:** Diagnosed the Next/Turbopack `Can't resolve '@tiptap/core'` build error without fixing it. TipTap `3.31.4` is present in both committed dependency files, but absent from local `node_modules` and its installed-tree lock. `npm ls` and `require.resolve` independently confirm the missing installation. Local install metadata is older than the pulled package files, so the root cause is a stale local dependency tree after Git synchronization, not a missing import declaration or missing lockfile entry. No application or dependency changes were made.
